- **Prediction** - (iii) abstain-on-brand wins: photos rarely carry legible brand text, document context already names the product (H220's anchoring), and asserting identity from pixels is exactly the task VLMs fake - the guard aligns the modality with what it actually knows, the same lesson as the abstention doctrine everywhere else in the engine
- **Acceptance bar** - both numbers per guard on the frozen H218 gold (same 12 photos + the 3 hallucination cases as the must-fix set); refuted if every guard that fixes precision destroys recall below 0.80 (the photo class then ships description-only-with-anchoring or enters the gap ledger)
- **Experiment** - rides the H218 harness and frozen gold (cheap: 12 photos x 3 guards on the GPU-0 Qwen setup); queue with the R21 next tier

## R27 extension - the type-label root cause (registered 2026-07-08, post-precision-arms)

The precision arms' loss anatomy raises the registered question: every true-merge loss was a cross-type alias (Flow Meter[Accessory] = Flowmeter[Feature]) whose CONFLICTING extractor type labels triggered the judge's type-mismatch rule - the labels are extraction noise, not identity evidence, and no adjudicator behavior can fix what the labels poison.

### R27-H292 Type-conflict is extractor noise - reconcile labels before judging identity

- **Grounding** - H282's stratified collapse (TM-preservation 0.29 / 0.00 exactly and only on the type-conflict strata) localizes the failure to the type axis; the campaign owns three reconciliation candidates: multi-label union (an entity may be Accessory AND Feature - the cross-type resolver already emits multi_facet decisions), type-blind judging (strip type labels from the judge context, decide on name/description/provenance), and cured-ontology label arbitration (the hybrid consolidation machinery re-types the pair before the judge sees it); H229 closed the door on fixing this by extraction determinism, so reconciliation must happen post-hoc
- **Hypothesis** - at least one reconciliation lifts single-shot true-merge preservation on the type-conflict strata from 0.29/0.00 to >= 0.75 while false-merge detection stays >= 95% overall - restoring the judge as a safe standalone instrument (and shrinking H290's reliance on demotion as the safety net)
- **Prediction** - type-blind judging wins cheapest: the judge's own reasoning cites the type conflict as its split reason, and names/descriptions/shared provenance carry the true signal; multi-label union wins on principle but needs resolver plumbing
- **Acceptance bar** - both numbers per candidate on the frozen 127-pair labels (the type-conflict strata are the must-fix set); refuted if every candidate that fixes preservation drags detection under 95% - the demote-don't-delete composition then stands as the permanent form and type labels are recorded as identity-hostile
- **Experiment** - cheap: rides the frozen labels + warm LLM cache (results/r27_precision/), 61 type-conflict pairs x 3 candidates; queue on the local endpoint behind the running batch
- **Result** - (executor 2026-07-08, [`type_reconciliation_h292.ipynb`](../../notebooks/type_reconciliation_h292.ipynb) / [`type-reconciliation-h292-20260708T100715Z.json`](../../reports/type-reconciliation-h292-20260708T100715Z.json); H282 context reproduced byte-exact, 127/127 warm-cache verified, only the type treatment varied) type-blind judging: TC-strata TM-preservation **0.750** (exactly the bar, 6/8) at FM-detection **95.05%** (clears 95% by one pair) and 480 tok/pair - BOTH clauses pass, prediction confirmed (cheapest candidate wins). Multi-label 0.625 FAIL; cured-ontology arbitration 0.250 FAIL at double price (the arbiter reconciles only 11/61 TC pairs to equal types - true cross-type aliases legitimately carry different labels, so re-typing cannot dissolve the conflict). Honesty: type-blind ADMITS 3 extra false merges vs H282 (5 vs 2) - stripping labels blinds the judge on pairs where the label difference WAS the evidence. Caveat: TC-true denominator is 8 pairs; single-pair resolution
- **Verdict** - CONFIRMED at the bars, NOT SHIPPED on the composition arithmetic: under H290's demote-don't-delete, H282's over-splits are structurally COSTLESS (demoted edges keep full render value per H288) while type-blind's 3 extra missed false merges remain as SAME_AS poison - the asymmetry inverts the tradeoff, so the shipped court stays H282 + demotion (98.0% detection) and H292's preservation gain is redundant with the demotion safety net. Standing lesson recorded: type labels ARE identity evidence on this corpus (removing them costs detection); the H290 composition is confirmed as the dominant form from two independent directions

## R28 - graph maintenance and self-healing under the stateless-session kernel (user-directed, pre-registered 2026-07-09)

The user directs a maintenance / self-healing round with one load-bearing constraint: consecutive KGF sessions may be triggered STATELESSLY - no local process state, no guaranteed local files, no in-memory carryover - so the shared Neo4j graph is the ONLY state that survives between sessions, and a foreign session on another host must reconstruct everything it needs from it. This kernel makes in-graph storage the substrate, not an option, and it kills every arm that would argue the maintenance state should live outside the graph (a stateless foreign session would then have no state at all). Two more binding constraints: token/compute cost of rebuild or repair is NOT a verdict gate (budget is abundant; falsifiers key on correctness, fidelity, safety, and cross-scenario generalization, never on cost), and the naive baseline is the shipped passive detector - session-local, ingest-drift-only, fires warnings and never acts, forgets everything at session end (the measured pathology: 52 drift.warning across 11 logs, 0 recure / 0 rebuild, even at js_divergence 0.5079, 3.4x the 0.15 rebuild threshold, because its window lives in RAM and resets each session). Every hypothesis reports its predicted delta against that baseline.

The round runs seven persona-driven groups: (A) active usage/eval triggers vs passive drift, (B) graph-health metric time-series, (C) in-graph metric + maintenance STATE, (D) anomaly detection in the metric series, (E) self-healing - both whether it helps at all and the repair-strategy x scenario matrix, (F) provenance in the graph, and (G) statelessness solvability - whether graph-only-state maintenance can actually be solved. Groups A/B/D are the first-pass slate; C and E were regenerated under the stateless kernel and F/G added; the first-pass C arms that argued "in-graph is useless, the external store wins" (H301-H304) are SUPERSEDED and not carried - a stateless foreign session has no external store to read - and three marginal first-pass E arms (H309, H310, H312) fold into H320/H321, leaving those ordinals unissued. Discipline mirrors R27: every reconstruction/replay arm runs OFFLINE and FREE against the 11 frozen event logs plus the frozen reports/artifacts (no GPU, no live graph); the concurrency arms (Neo4j-write interleavings) and the repair/re-extraction arms queue behind the Phase-3 rebuild, which frees neo4j4 and the GPU. Three head-to-heads are deliberately kept adversarial - snapshot-vs-fold (H315/H332 vs H331, discriminated by H332's permuted-ingestion-order test, run first), lease-vs-CAS (H324 vs H334 on one mid-action-death harness), and provenance-minimality (H328 vs H327/H329) - and the round reopens R26's H273-vs-H274 caching head-to-head (H336), because content-addressing that was optional for an external store becomes mandatory in-graph.

**Group A - active usage/eval triggers vs passive drift**

### R28-H293 CONTRARIAN - active recall probe catches what passive drift misses

- **Grounding** - duplicate/mention accumulation and soft-link decay grow valid entities without remapping types, so remap stays sub-threshold and JSD stays low while recall@16 and fully_covered fall; the passive detector is structurally silent on the failed axis, an active retrieval probe measures it directly
- **Hypothesis** - the passive all-3-consecutive drift detector is decoupled from retrieval-quality degradation, so an active recall@16 gold-battery probe catches a graph-quality collapse it misses entirely
- **Prediction** - passive drift alarm count on h240b-mention (recall 0.6667 vs enum 0.7292, fully_covered 14 vs 17) = 0 (confirmed: 0 drift.warning in both h240b logs); active probe flags it at a -0.05 gold-battery threshold; on flat h241 v1/v2 (recall 0.7708) the active probe adds 0 false alarms where passive already fired 3 benign drift.warnings each
- **Acceptance bar** - REFUTED (H50/H59 sufficiency survives into maintenance regime) unless >=1 recall-drop event (recall drop >=0.05 or fully_covered drop >=2 on same corpus) has 0 passive alarms AND the active probe flags it, with 0 active false alarms on the flat h241 arms; h240b-mention already stands as one counterexample
- **Experiment** - FREE replay, no GPU: pair reports/h240b-{enum,mention}-stats.json + h241-v{1,2}-stats.json with the matching event logs; set-diff quality-degradation events, count drift.warning per log, simulate the recall@16 trigger at -0.05, tabulate passive-miss vs active-hit and active false alarms on the flat arms

### R28-H294 FOLLOWER - read-only probe battery leads the schema-only detector

- **Grounding** - a false-merge collapse of hub entities or an attachment-edge drop degrades recall@16 / orphan_rate / duplicate_name_density without moving the type-frequency distribution, so passive stays below threshold by construction while the battery fires immediately
- **Hypothesis** - a read-only recall+traversal probe battery alarms on maintenance-worthy graph-quality degradation earlier than the schema-only passive drift detector
- **Prediction** - probe-battery lead time >=3 mutation-events at trigger precision >=0.9; passive js_divergence/remap stays sub-warn across the full seeded degradation while recall@16 falls >=5 pts
- **Acceptance bar** - REFUTED unless lead time >=3 mutation-events (or >=1 doc) AND battery added false-alarm count on the benign full-remap class = 0; lead time 0 or any benign false alarm kills it
- **Experiment** - br-A FREE kill-gate: pair 6 frozen builds (h212-v2/rerun, h241-v1/v2, h158-v1/v2) terminal js_divergence/remap vs report retrieval quality, Spearman |r|<0.4 confirms decoupling and the arm proceeds, |r|>=0.6 kills it; br-B seeded arm: in-memory read-only render mutation (H288/H254 precedent, no rebuild) on frozen h212/h241, inject K in {2,5,10} hub false-merges + drop M in {10,25,50} edges, replay both detectors' logic, record mutations-to-alarm

### R28-H295 DEFLATIONIST - the dead type-burst counter reproduces the fire schedule free

- **Grounding** - post-cure remap_rate and JSD are both driven by one latent (new types outside the cured set); the dead recure_type_burst counter reads it directly while the probe reads it through two derived statistics and a rolling window, so the probe is an invertible transform of the counter, not an independent signal
- **Hypothesis** - the passive detector's remap+JSD apparatus re-encodes post-cure new-type emergence, so any R28 active-probe trigger built atop it adds no timing information the free counter lacks
- **Prediction** - Jaccard(counter fires, detector fires) >=0.9; Spearman(windowed type_emerged, remap_rate) >=0.7; >=1 benign-remap doc where remap is high but type_emerged==0 and the counter abstains (134 type_emerged, 46 drift.warning, 32 curing.metrics on kgf)
- **Acceptance bar** - REFUTED unless Jaccard >=0.9 AND counter false-alarm count <= detector's on the same post-cure stream; the causal probe story survives only if a detector fire the counter misses also tracks a real subsequent quality drop
- **Experiment** - FREE replay: logs/kgf-events.jsonl (46 drift.warning, 134 type_emerged, 4 curing.cured) + h212-v2 + phase3-rebuild logs; per post-cure doc align count(type_emerged since last cure) against drift.warning remap/jsd/action, compute Jaccard, Spearman, flag remap-high/type_emerged==0 docs; baseline = live DriftSettings (remap 0.3, rebuild_jsd 0.15, window 3)

### R28-H296 DEFLATIONIST - a contentless cadence timer fails the false-alarm guardrail

- **Grounding** - dose-response separation: a real trigger co-moves with type churn (a content dose), a timer has zero dose so its fires distribute independently of drift and contaminate stable stretches
- **Hypothesis** - a contentless fixed doc-cadence timer FAILS the H59 false-alarm guardrail, bracketing the trigger's minimal signal: the H295 type-burst counter, not a bare timer, is the floor
- **Prediction** - timer places >=30% of fires on type_emerged==0 windows (23/32 recorded fluid docs were tar==0, so stable stretches dominate) vs the counter's ~0%, pushing the timer above the H59 budget while the counter stays within it
- **Acceptance bar** - timer refuted-as-trigger unless its false-alarm rate on stable docs <= the H295 counter's; the expected failure is the positive result confirming the counter's content signal cannot be dropped
- **Experiment** - FREE replay, same substrate as H295: simulate cadence N over the post-cure doc sequence, count the share of timer fires whose trailing window has type_emerged==0, compare to the counter's and the live detector's false-alarm share, no GPU

**Group B - graph-health metric time-series**

### R28-H297 FOLLOWER - cure-exit panel as a per-doc time-series leads drift.py

- **Grounding** - a shakier cure (under-consolidated singletons, high JS variance, low Chao1 coverage) produces more downstream type remaps, so panel state at curing.cured foreshadows the remap/JSD spikes drift.py only detects after its 3-doc window fills
- **Hypothesis** - emitting the cure-exit StabilityMetrics panel as a persisted per-doc time-series gives a drift early-warning that leads drift.py's own alarm at near-zero cost
- **Prediction** - cure-exit js_divergence_var and singleton-fraction correlate positively (Spearman rho >=0.5) with the count of drift.warning in the following STABLE segment; panel crosses threshold >=1 doc before drift.py's first warning on the majority of episodes
- **Acceptance bar** - REFUTED unless Spearman rho >=0.5 across >=10 pooled cure->stable segments AND false-pre-warning count <= the naive baseline's standing false-alarm count on the same segments
- **Experiment** - FREE replay: over logs/{kgf,h212-v2,phase3-rebuild,h240b-enum,h241-v1,h157,h158-v2}-events.jsonl take the last curing.metrics before each curing.cured, join to the drift.warning count before the next cure, pool ~10-15 segments, compute Spearman and the false-pre-warning tally against the H59 floor

### R28-H298 MECHANIST - the health panel is near rank-deficient; JSD is vehicle not cause

- **Grounding** - all ~18 metrics are closed-form functions of one frequency vector, so the panel has low effective rank; JSD decays to ~0 at convergence for every run regardless of final recall, so it cannot discriminate end-state payoff while coverage saturation can - the drift engine fires on JSD only because JSD was the instrumented lever
- **Hypothesis** - the 20+ metric health panel's recall-predictive content collapses onto one coverage/novelty undercurrent, and JSD is the vehicle the drift engine triggers on, not the cause
- **Prediction** - PCA over 129 rows: top-2 PCs explain >=80% of variance; terminal coverage-channel |Spearman| >=0.5 with mean_recall and >=0.9x best-single-metric R^2; terminal JSD |Spearman| <0.3 and its partial correlation with recall given coverage <0.15
- **Acceptance bar** - REFUTED unless top-2 PC variance >=0.80 AND coverage recall-R^2 >= 0.9x best-single-metric R^2 AND JSD partial-correlation-given-coverage <0.15; guardrail: a coverage-triggered alarm backtest adds 0 benign false fires over the JSD/remap baseline
- **Experiment** - FREE replay, no GPU: br1 = 129 curing.metrics rows (18 metrics) across the 11 event logs, br2 = paired report mean_recall (0.667-0.875); build the 129x18 matrix -> correlation + PCA effective rank, per-run terminal univariate Spearman vs mean_recall, partial correlations both directions, leave-one-run-out stability on the winning channel

### R28-H299 SCOUT - relationship-type entropy monitor sees the axis the baseline is blind to

- **Grounding** - relation surface is extracted more permissively than governed (gleaning recalls new rel types faster than consolidation merges them), so the entity distribution can stay stationary while the relation vocabulary explodes; an entity-only detector cannot observe the relation axis
- **Hypothesis** - a windowed relationship-type entropy monitor detects relation-vocabulary proliferation the entity-type drift baseline is structurally blind to, firing at least one build before any existing trigger
- **Prediction** - on the scorecard series (2.22/6.22/3.06/0.0/2.84) the 6.22 build shows delta relationship_type_entropy about +4.0 nat while entity-type JSD/remap stayed sub-threshold (no drift.decision emitted); a rel-entropy CUSUM at matched ARL0 fires on that build and none of the stable ones
- **Acceptance bar** - REFUTED unless the rel-entropy monitor flags >=1 real proliferation build (delta relationship_type_entropy >1.5 nat) the entity-type baseline scored none, with 0 false alarms across stable-entropy builds (H59 budget held)
- **Experiment** - FREE replay: reports/scorecard-*.json series + logs/*-events.jsonl; recompute a windowed rel-type-entropy CUSUM and set-diff its firings against emitted drift decisions; per-doc rel-TYPE strings are absent from event logs, so the build-level snapshot trend is the free kill-gate and a per-doc emit + rebuild arm queues behind Phase-3

### R28-H300 SCOUT - temporal ECE drift on the resolver posterior stream

- **Grounding** - posterior reliability depends on prior * LR_desc * LR_emb * LR_cooc terms whose base rates move with corpus drift; entity-type JSD is uncorrelated with posterior miscalibration, so schema drift and calibration drift are independent axes and the current panel monitors only the former
- **Hypothesis** - a temporal ECE / reliability drift monitor over the resolver posterior stream triggers a calibration refit before miscalibration degrades merge decisions - a self-healing axis KGF does not model
- **Prediction** - across logged epochs the resolution.merge posterior mean shifts measurably (kgf mean 0.796 vs h240b-enum 0.545, delta 0.25 >> 0.05) with no drift.decision in either window - an unmodelled silently-decaying calibration axis
- **Acceptance bar** - REFUTED unless the posterior-distribution ECE proxy drifts >=0.05 between logged epochs with 0 corresponding drift alarms, establishing an uncaught calibration-health axis the naive baseline cannot see
- **Experiment** - FREE replay: logs/kgf + h241-* + h240b-* resolution.merge posterior fields; compute a per-epoch reliability proxy (binned mean posterior vs merge-acceptance rate per bin), compare across epochs, cross-reference drift.decision; true-label ECE against the H157 identity set queues behind Phase-3

**Group C - in-graph metric + maintenance state (stateless-mandatory; supersedes first-pass H301-H304)**

### R28-H315 FOLLOWER - append-only MetricSnapshot reconstructs the drift window verdict-for-verdict

- **Grounding** - the drift window is a session-local Python list that starts empty, so a single-doc stateless session never accumulates the window and _evaluate short-circuits to none (the 52-warnings/0-actions pathology); sourcing the list from an ORDER BY over a persisted ledger makes the window span all post-cure documents across sessions. Stateless: the window becomes a pure Cypher ORDER BY ingest_seq LIMIT query over immutable nodes, so a foreign host derives the identical window with zero local state
- **Hypothesis** - append-only :MetricSnapshot nodes (one per ingested document: full StabilityMetrics vector + type-frequency dict + remap/contradiction rates, ordered by a graph-assigned ingest_seq under a :CureState epoch baseline) let any stateless session reconstruct the DriftDetector window by query and reproduce, verdict-for-verdict, the sequence a single continuous stateful run produces - firing the recure/rebuild the session-local baseline never fires
- **Prediction** - replaying the 11-log post-cure stream as N stateless single-doc sessions yields a verdict list element-identical to one continuous in-memory run, and fires >=1 rebuild at the js=0.5079 doc vs the baseline's 0 actions
- **Acceptance bar** - verdict-for-verdict parity across the full stream AND every continuous-run recure/rebuild reproduced by the corresponding stateless session; falsified by any document index where the stateless-reconstructed verdict differs from the continuous-run verdict, or a stateless session that fails to emit an action the continuous run emits at the same index
- **Experiment** - FREE replay, no GPU: reconstruct :MetricSnapshot rows from the curing.metrics + drift events across the 11 logs, replay the window as N single-doc stateless sessions vs one continuous run, diff the verdict lists; the in-graph node write + from_graph query validate behind Phase-3

### R28-H316 FOLLOWER - MERGE (doc,epoch) + monotonic seq makes the window exactly-once under concurrency

- **Grounding** - once the window lives in the shared graph, naive appends double-count a re-ingested document and corrupt JSD/remap means, and wall-clock ordering lets clock skew across hosts reorder the window; an idempotent MERGE collapses duplicates to one contribution and a graph-issued monotonic sequence gives a total order independent of any host clock. Stateless: the MERGE key and the graph-resident counter make any concurrent or later session rebuild the same exactly-once, same-ordered window with no local clock or dedup state
- **Hypothesis** - MERGE-keying each snapshot on (document_id, cure_epoch) with a graph-issued monotonic ingest_seq (not wall-clock) makes the reconstructed window exactly-once and order-stable under concurrent/overlapping ingest, so its verdict equals the serial-execution verdict
- **Prediction** - under K concurrent sessions replaying an overlapping document set the reconstructed window has exactly one snapshot per (document,epoch) and its verdict equals the deduplicated serial replay; a naive append design double-counts and diverges, and a timestamp-only sort fires a spurious or missed trigger
- **Acceptance bar** - no duplicate (document,epoch) contribution and verdict identical to serial replay under every interleaving tested; falsified by an interleaving that duplicates a contribution or diverges from serial replay, or if plain timestamp order already reproduces the stateful trigger for all interleavings (the causal chain then buys nothing)
- **Experiment** - Neo4j-write concurrency harness on neo4j4 -> queues behind Phase-3; a free offline replay pre-checks the dedup/ordering logic against the 11 logs first

### R28-H317 MECHANIST - CUSUM over the reconstructed series fires on drift diluted across short sessions

- **Grounding** - the boolean gate demands `window` CONSECUTIVE breaches inside one session's list, so a stateless 1-2 doc session can never assemble a consecutive run and evidence resets every session; CUSUM accumulates fractional deviation across sessions, so three sessions each contributing one mild breach sum to a crossing. Stateless: the accumulator is a deterministic left-fold over ordered MetricSnapshots keyed to a cure_epoch, any session recomputes S_i exactly and the cached memo is discardable
- **Hypothesis** - a CUSUM change-point statistic (S_i=max(0,S_{i-1}+(jsd_i-(mu0+k))), fire when S_i>h, mu0=median jsd of the epoch's first W snapshots) folded over the graph-reconstructed post-cure JSD series fires recure/rebuild that the consecutive-breach boolean structurally cannot when drift evidence is diluted across many short stateless sessions
- **Prediction** - on the CPAP replay partitioned into single-doc stateless sessions CUSUM fires recure >=1 doc earlier than the reconstructed boolean and >=1 vs the baseline's 0, and it also catches a synthetic slow-ramp (each step sub-threshold) the boolean never catches
- **Acceptance bar** - detects the documented drift under single-doc session partitioning where the consecutive-breach detector scores zero, and generalizes to a slow-ramp drift the boolean never catches; falsified if CUSUM recomputed from the series diverges from the memo (non-reconstructable) or fires no earlier than the boolean on thin-session partitions
- **Experiment** - FREE replay, no GPU: fold CUSUM over the reconstructed JSD series from the 11 logs, partition into single-doc sessions, race vs the boolean and vs a seeded slow-ramp

### R28-H318 DEFLATIONIST - CAS version-guard on the KGFControl singleton stops last-writer-wins clobber

- **Grounding** - current write_control MERGEs then blind-SETs the whole serialized blob, so two sessions that each read state, append one window entry, and write back the full blob will have the second overwrite the first's append (DEF-7), reproducing the window-dies symptom even after H315; CAS serializes the writes so the loser re-reads and re-appends against fresh state. Stateless: the version integer lives on the node, any foreign session serializes its write against the read version
- **Hypothesis** - the single :KGFControl node written with a blind SET c+=props is a last-writer-wins clobber point; a compare-and-set version guard (MATCH (c:KGFControl {version:$expected}) SET c+=$props, c.version=c.version+1, raise on zero rows) is the minimal addition genuinely required so concurrent writers cannot silently destroy each other's maintenance state
- **Prediction** - two concurrent waves both writing control: without the guard the persisted drift window is shorter than the sum of per-writer appends (observable lost update); with it every append survives and fsm_state never regresses
- **Acceptance bar** - a concurrent two-writer test shows persisted drift-window length equals total appends (zero lost updates) and zero corrupted fsm_state transitions; redundant (falsified) if all reconstructable series move to append-only nodes and the only residual KGFControl writes are idempotent scalar FSM transitions tolerant of last-writer-wins
- **Experiment** - Neo4j-write two-writer harness on neo4j4 -> queues behind Phase-3; a free code-read of write_control confirms the blind-SET blob pattern now

**Group D - anomaly detection in the metric series**

### R28-H305 TRANSFER - multivariate PCA monitor catches correlation-break anomalies univariate misses

- **Grounding** - MPCA treats each ingest as a batch and each per-doc metric vector as a trajectory sample; Q/SPE measures distance OUT of the reference subspace, so an anomaly whose every component stays within its historical [p5,p95] band but whose joint direction is novel produces a large residual - a class univariate detectors cannot represent
- **Hypothesis** - a PCA-based process monitor (Hotelling T-squared on retained scores + Q/SPE residual) over the joint per-doc curing.metrics vector detects correlation-break anomalies that keep every single metric in-band, which the univariate all-3-consecutive detector misses by construction
- **Prediction** - at matched ARL0~=485, Q/SPE flags >=80% of seeded correlation-break anomalies (univariate miss 100%) with 0 false alarms on the ~129 pooled real vectors
- **Acceptance bar** - REFUTED unless Q/SPE detection >=80% on seeded joint-break anomalies AND 0 false alarms on the ~129 real curing.metrics vectors at ARL0>=485; kill-gate: if 0 real joint-only outliers exist AND effective rank <=3, the monitor solves a non-problem
- **Experiment** - FREE replay, numpy-only: pool 129 per-doc curing.metrics vectors across the 11 logs, fit reference PCA to 95% variance, set T-squared/Q limits by chi-square/F approximation, bisect to ARL0~=485 on the clean stream, seed correlation-break anomalies by within-band permutation of an anti-correlated pair, race detection vs all-3-consecutive at 3 seed magnitudes

### R28-H306 CONTRARIAN - the anomaly-detection ceiling is empty on this corpus

- **Grounding** - a detector's benefit ceiling equals the count of genuine maintenance events its metric can separate; if that set is empty the ceiling collapses to zero benefit while any non-empty flag set incurs false-alarm cost against the H59 budget
- **Hypothesis** - even a hindsight-optimal single-metric threshold on the stability panel cannot beat the passive rule, because the set of true maintenance-worthy events on this corpus is empty; the ceiling of panel anomaly detection is <= do-nothing minus false-alarm cost
- **Prediction** - grid-searching thresholds over jsd/entropy_delta/type_accumulation/singletons/chao1_coverage produces no threshold that flags a material forward-mass block (none exists); the hindsight-optimal detector is the trivial never-fire detector, matching the passive rule's realized 0 decisions
- **Acceptance bar** - attack LANDS if across all runs the material-forward-mass-block set is empty (oracle TP = 0); REFUTED if any run contains >=1 material block a panel metric threshold separates from benign spikes - then anomaly detection has a real ceiling and the net-negative claim is regime-narrow
- **Experiment** - FREE replay: over the curing.metrics logs label each doc maintenance-worthy iff it introduces novel types carrying >=5% of subsequent observation mass OR triggers a drift.decision (measured 0), grid-search single-metric thresholds for max TP-FP, report max achievable TP; zero LLM/GPU

### R28-H307 MECHANIST - benign warnings are small-doc quantization noise on the remap arm

- **Grounding** - remap_rate = remapped/entities has a tiny denominator for light docs so its variance scales as ~1/entities; the 46 observed warnings fire on docs of 2-14 entities (mean 6.7) vs corpus p90=23, so the confound is light-doc quantization, opposite-signed to the assumed heavy-document driver
- **Hypothesis** - the naive detector's benign warnings are driven by small-document quantization noise on the remap-rate arm, not heavy documents, so conditioning that arm on a document entity-count floor (>=15) eliminates them losslessly
- **Prediction** - an entity floor of 15 (below corpus p90=23, above the max warning-doc size of 14) removes 46/46 (100%) of current warnings; all-3-consecutive genuine escalations unaffected because 0 logs ever escalated and the JSD arm is untouched
- **Acceptance bar** - REFUTED unless the floor removes >=90% of the 46 benign warnings on kgf AND suppresses 0 recure/rebuild escalations across all logs (H59 guardrail two-sided)
- **Experiment** - FREE replay: over logs/kgf (46 drift.warnings, 0 escalations) + h241-v1/v2 (3 warnings each), re-run DriftDetector offline pairing each drift.warning with the preceding document.completed entity count, recompute the warning set under a remap-arm entity floor of 15, count removed-benign vs suppressed-genuine; no GPU

**Group E - self-healing: (i) does it help at all, (ii) repair-strategy x scenario (supersedes first-pass H309/H310/H312)**

### R28-H308 HERETICAL - delete the active self-heal, append-only + abstain holds recall

- **Grounding** - in an append-only store compaction and re-materialization are pure write-amplification that change read latency and space, never the projected answer; KGF rebuild/recure is the same reorganization but KGF queries have no read-latency problem it solves, and H288 proved the render already reaches counterpart neighborhoods through the soft hop
- **Hypothesis** - deleting the active recall-side self-heal channel (recure_type_burst + drift-triggered rebuild + H272 micro-pass) and running pure append-only + abstain holds recall@16 and same_as_precision within noise while removing 100% of self-heal compute
- **Prediction** - recall@16 delta of append-only vs full self-heal <=2 pts (observed ceiling +0.0), same_as_precision delta within 0.2 tol, self-heal compute reduced 100%, drift false-alarm count 0
- **Acceptance bar** - REFUTED unless append-only holds recall@16 within 2 pts AND same_as_precision within 0.2 of the self-heal policy across the replay corpus; a single recorded would-have-rebuilt/recured document whose measured post-action recall or precision beats the append-only counterfactual by >2 pts refutes the heresy
- **Experiment** - FREE replay: re-parse all logs' drift events (confirm 0 recure + 0 rebuild, max-JSD doc 0.5079), reconstruct the DriftDetector verdict per post-cure doc, label each doc's would-have-action, and for every doc labeled recure/rebuild (expected none) compare downstream recall@16 / same_as_precision against the append-only+abstain counterfactual under the H288 defer-band arithmetic

### R28-H311 FOLLOWER - the targeted-intervention menu beats the rebuild floor >=3x per token

- **Grounding** - the rebuild action re-ingests the whole corpus at census scale (H211: 2.14M census tokens) to recover recall that is 78%-concentrated in one doc; a targeted micro-pass on that neighborhood buys the same recall for one doc's residue scan (H211 knee 120k tokens ~5.6% of census), so the recovered-recall-per-token ratio is dominated by the denominator collapse
- **Hypothesis** - the automatic targeted-intervention menu dominates the MANDATORY naive floor (the passive detector's full rebuild) by >=3x on recovered recall per token, the metric H271 never measured
- **Prediction** - targeted-menu recovered-recall-per-token >=3x the full-rebuild floor: >=90% of rebuild recall gain (>=36 of ~40 recoverable flips) at <=20% of rebuild tokens (<=~430k vs ~2.14M census-equivalent)
- **Acceptance bar** - REFUTED unless the targeted menu reaches >=90% of the full-rebuild recall gain at <=20% of rebuild tokens (>=3x recall-per-token); also refuted if the missed-probe mass does NOT localize to a repairable neighborhood, or the trigger false-alarm rate exceeds the H59 budget
- **Experiment** - FREE simulation, no GPU: full-rebuild recall gain and token cost from census anchors in unranked-residue-h211 (census_tokens 2140060, knee 119843) + clean-recall-h212 baseline; targeted-menu gain from H271 adjudication (dw_uniform_ratio_by_budget: 39 flips at K=1) in usage-coupling-gates-r26; price the micro-pass at the H252/H272 <=0.2x re-ingest constant, compute recall-per-token for both and the ratio

### R28-H313 CONTRARIAN - do-nothing is regime-narrow; completeness gaps need a micro-pass

- **Grounding** - soft links connect nodes that already exist, but a gold absent from the graph has nothing to link to, so no attachment/identity lever (H268/H288/H290) can render it; only re-extraction of the source span creates the node, so H288's +0.0 is a property of the defer-band population, not of recall failures at large
- **Hypothesis** - the do-nothing-dominates doctrine is regime-narrow: most of the benchmark's recall failures are extraction-completeness gaps (in_graph=false) that soft-links and resolution structurally cannot render, so an active micro-pass re-extraction on the in_graph=false residue has non-zero marginal value do-nothing cannot reach
- **Prediction** - free probe already run: of 8 failed golds (in_top16_seeds=false) 6 are in_graph=false & in_source_chunks=true (75%) and 2 are in_graph=true; do-nothing covers 0 of the in_graph=false residue and a GLiNER-audited micro-pass (H260 carrier recall 95.2%) recovers >=40% -> >=+2 pts over the H268 baseline H288 declared saturated
- **Acceptance bar** - REFUTED unless (a) the micro-pass over the in_graph=false residue lifts wide-set recall >=2 pts over the H268 soft-link do-nothing baseline AND (b) aim-precision >=0.8; if the in_graph=false share is <40% of failed golds or micro-pass recovers <40% of it, do-nothing-dominates is upheld
- **Experiment** - br FREE replay over clean-recall-h212 forensics (per-gold in_graph/in_source_chunks/in_top16_seeds) is the kill-gate and population split, already run (6/8 failed golds in_graph=false, clears the 40% bar); the GPU arm re-extracts the in_graph=false chunks via the H252/H260 micro-pass under the pinned H207/H212 harness and queues behind Phase-3, which regenerates forensics to widen N beyond the 8 unique probes

### R28-H314 CONTRARIAN - no query-time signal separates repairable from soft-link failures

- **Grounding** - abstention posterior and orphan/degree signals are computed from the graph AS BUILT, so a gold never extracted contributes nothing to those signals and the trigger cannot distinguish missing-because-unreachable (soft-link fixes) from missing-because-unextracted (re-extraction fixes) - aim is impossible without already knowing the gold
- **Hypothesis** - even granting the in_graph=false repairable residue exists, no query-time self-eval signal separates repairable (in_graph=false) from soft-link (in_graph=true) failures, so any deployed repair trigger fires ambiently and is dominated by do-nothing on cost
- **Prediction** - separation AUC of any recorded feature (score, in_top16_seeds, source-count, abstention posterior) for predicting in_graph on the forensics golds <0.7; wasted-fire share (triggers on in_graph=true) >=50%, matching the measured 2/8-in_graph-true-among-failures split
- **Acceptance bar** - REFUTED (aimed trigger exists) if some recorded feature separates in_graph true/false at AUC >=0.7; if AUC <0.7 the attack lands - the trigger is ambient, wastes >=50% of budget on soft-link territory, and do-nothing wins on cost per H290
- **Experiment** - br FREE replay: over the forensics golds fit/evaluate whether any per-gold field or census feature predicts in_graph (AUC), and separately test query-log abstention/gap-ledger events for a pre-repair separator; existing reports/logs, no GPU; widening requires Phase-3 to regenerate forensics (current N=8 unique probes)

### R28-H319 MECHANIST - the strategy x scenario matrix is non-collapsible

- **Grounding** - rebuild re-runs the identical extraction stochastics that MINTED the duplicate (H107: 71% of identity failures are extraction variance), so it re-samples the same variance and often re-creates the split, while alias-synthesis writes a SAME_AS edge invariant to extraction variance; symmetrically alias-synthesis only adds edges and cannot re-partition the type distribution, so only recure/rebuild moves JSD - the mechanisms are orthogonal, so no operator is universal. Stateless: both statistics are recomputed from the graph on entry, so a session re-derives which scenario it faces and picks the diagonal operator with no inherited state
- **Hypothesis** - a controlled 2x2 (identity gap vs schema-drift, crossed with alias_synthesis vs full_rebuild) shows alias_synthesis strictly beats full_rebuild on identity gaps and strictly loses on drift; no single strategy, rebuild included, dominates, so a universal repair is provably wrong
- **Prediction** - alias_synthesis drops unresolved-pair count >=80% while leaving JSD unchanged; full_rebuild moves JSD toward the cured baseline but leaves unresolved-pair count within noise; the two effect vectors are anti-correlated across the two statistics
- **Acceptance bar** - each operator improves ONLY its diagonal scenario's statistic (>=80% relative reduction) and fails or regresses the off-diagonal one; falsified if full_rebuild closes identity as well as alias_synthesis, or alias moves JSD, or any operator improves both statistics simultaneously
- **Experiment** - GPU + graph: alias_synthesis is graph-only (free) but full_rebuild re-ingests and the identity scenario needs a real graph -> queues behind Phase-3, which supplies the fresh graph; the identity-scenario statistic (cosine>=0.9 unresolved pairs) can be pre-measured free on a frozen reference graph

### R28-H320 MECHANIST - first-class Gap nodes make the repair worklist graph-reconstructable

- **Grounding** - moving diagnosis into the graph as queryable Gap nodes means a foreign stateless session reconstructs the entire outstanding worklist with one query and knows both WHICH operator and WHERE; because each probe writes a distinct kind bound to one operator, the router itself is the generalization mechanism (one schema, one loop, no per-scenario driver code). Stateless: MATCH (g:Gap) WHERE g.valid_to IS NULL returns operator+target, and a session killed mid-repair loses zero intent
- **Hypothesis** - a stateless session reconstructs the repair worklist purely from the graph by materializing first-class (:Gap {kind,target_id,statistic}) nodes from a fixed Cypher anomaly-probe battery (identity->alias_synthesis, fidelity->quote_backfill, completeness->targeted_reextract, drift->local_recure, contradiction->temporal_invalidate), each kind a FUNCTION to exactly one operator, closed bitemporally when the re-run probe returns clean
- **Prediction** - probe->route->repair->re-probe closes >=80% of open Gaps in-run; a cold stateless session reconstructs a byte-identical open-Gap set (same target_ids, kinds), and the reconstructed drift JSD reproduces 0.5079 and now fires an action, not a warning
- **Acceptance bar** - cold-session open-Gap set identical to warm session AND >=80% of open Gaps closed by their routed operator with the re-run probe confirming closure and no untargeted-region regression; falsified if one operator must close two kinds, closing one Gap pushes another kind's statistic over threshold, the cold set differs from the warm set, or tags oscillate open->clear->open
- **Experiment** - graph + GPU (the probes read a live graph, some operators re-extract) -> queues behind Phase-3; the probe-battery Cypher and kind->operator routing table are code-authored and unit-tested free now

### R28-H321 CONTRARIAN - Demand nodes make completeness gaps a queryable repair address

- **Grounding** - Chao1/ACE yield a scalar coverage that says a graph is incomplete but names no missing item and points at no region; duplicates, conflicts, JSD deltas and contradictions are positive traces a session can locate, but a missing entity/edge is the absence of a trace and unlocatable by construction - attributing the deficit to a concrete region and persisting it as a Demand node converts the un-locatable into a graph address. Stateless: Demand nodes ARE the state a foreign session reads, coverage attribution recomputed from persisted Document/Chunk provenance - this is the in-graph move of R26's external H271 demand ledger
- **Hypothesis** - completeness gaps leave no positive, localizable trace, so the re-extraction/backfill cells are un-targetable from the shipped graph and the matrix silently collapses to "repair only what is already visible"; materializing negative space as Demand/Expectation nodes (per unfilled ontology slot, unresolved probe, per-region Chao1/ACE deficit) converts absence into a queryable repair address
- **Prediction** - delete N known entities from a fixed doc set: baseline detects and acts 0; the Demand design flags the affected region and routes the correct strategy on >=80% of injected gaps, and a second stateless session selects the same region/strategy from the graph alone
- **Acceptance bar** - graph-read diagnosis localizes >=80% of injected completeness gaps to the correct region AND names the correct strategy, identical across two independent stateless sessions; falsified if a session reading only the shipped graph already localizes injected completeness gaps to the right region and strategy at >=80% (materialization then unnecessary)
- **Experiment** - graph + GPU (delete-N then re-extract) -> queues behind Phase-3; the coverage-attribution-to-region computation is pre-validated free on a frozen graph

### R28-H322 CONTRARIAN - a bitemporal repair-provenance ledger stops repeat and oscillation

- **Grounding** - without an intervention-outcome edge, two identical graph states from "gap present, untried" and "gap present, re-extraction already failed" are indistinguishable to a graph-only reader, so a memoryless session must pick the same action; the model_code SAME_AS false-merge surface (73/127 edges) makes merge<->split oscillation the default failure, not a rare one. Stateless: the ledger is bitemporal intervention edges on existing versioned nodes, so a foreign session reconstructs the full attempt history and antagonist-lockout window from the graph
- **Hypothesis** - named repair strategies are not stateless-idempotent (a session reading only current graph cannot tell a fresh gap from one a prior session already tried and failed), so a bitemporal repair-provenance ledger (region, strategy applied, pre/post fingerprint delta, outcome verdict) conditions selection on attempt history, escalating on repeated-failure and forbidding the inverse strategy on the same region within a window
- **Prediction** - across 3 consecutive stateless sessions on a hard-to-fix gap: baseline and a ledger-free design repeat or reverse session 1's action on >=1 region; the ledger design records 0 verbatim repeats and 0 merge<->split oscillations with monotonic escalation
- **Acceptance bar** - 0 verbatim strategy repeats and 0 identity-merge<->contradiction-split oscillations over 3 sessions, with monotonic escalation on repeated failure; falsified if ledger-free sessions already converge over 3 sessions (current state already encodes attempt outcomes)
- **Experiment** - graph + repair harness (3 sequential sessions) -> queues behind Phase-3; the ledger schema + escalation/lockout logic authored and unit-tested free now

### R28-H323 HERETICAL - one source-grounded re-derivation primitive beats the whole strategy catalog

- **Grounding** - every gap type is one underlying fault (the current graph diverges from what re-extracting its source provenance now yields); because provenance nodes persist, that divergence is always computable and repairable by re-deriving from source, while the named strategies are lossy graph-only patches that mutate WITHOUT consulting source and can drift the graph further from ground truth. Stateless: the primitive resolves any suspect node to its re-derivation scope purely by walking MENTIONED_IN to Chunk/Document, no session memory or strategy table
- **Hypothesis** - a single source-grounded re-derivation primitive (the shipped Foundry.repair, re-extract a node's backing chunks under a focus and reconcile into STABLE), re-targeted from provenance, matches or beats every named scenario-specific repair strategy across all five gap scenarios - so the strategy x scenario catalog should be deleted, not built
- **Prediction** - on a seeded fault set spanning all five scenarios, provenance-scoped re-derivation reaches repair quality >= the best bespoke strategy per scenario, with zero scenarios where a source-faithful graph-only patch strictly dominates; baseline repairs 0 with one code path instead of five
- **Acceptance bar** - for each of the five seeded scenarios one re-derivation path achieves repair-correctness (post-repair node/edge equals source-faithful gold) at least equal to the best scenario-specific strategy, and no scenario found where a source-faithful graph-only patch strictly wins; falsified by a seeded fault a graph-only patch repairs faithfully and generalizably that full-scope re-derivation cannot (e.g. a cross-chunk alias the union re-extraction still fails to synthesize)
- **Experiment** - GPU + graph (re-extraction over seeded faults) -> queues behind Phase-3, directly against H319/H320/H321's bespoke operators on the same fault set

### R28-H324 SCOUT - a MaintenanceLease supplies the one non-reconstructable signal (liveness)

- **Grounding** - graph CONTENT records accumulated fact but cannot express liveness (whether a writer is active now vs crashed), and a missing entity/edge looks identical whether a true defect or a chunk still in flight (DEF-8: H240b left counts flat ~55min mid-document with ~16 dropped chunks); only a repeatedly-refreshed heartbeat encodes liveness, which is definitionally not a historical fact. Stateless: the lease is the one non-historical maintenance atom but lives in-graph, so every foreign host sees the same node, atomicity is Neo4j's transaction lock and TTL bounds a dead holder's blast radius
- **Hypothesis** - "who is writing/repairing right now" is the one signal NOT reconstructable from committed facts; a single in-graph MaintenanceLease (heartbeat/TTL, content-addressed region_key from a deterministic subgraph fingerprint) gives exactly-once repair actuation, crash reclaim, and distinguishes a live in-flight ingest region from a genuine gap
- **Prediction** - baseline never acts so contention is invisible; with K>=2 concurrent sessions on one over-threshold graph the rebuild count drops from K to exactly 1, a killed holder's lease is reclaimed within one TTL and maintenance resumes, and zero repairs mutate a live-heartbeat region
- **Acceptance bar** - under K>=2 concurrent stateless sessions exactly one rebuild executes (safety) AND a crashed holder's lease is reclaimed after TTL so maintenance is never permanently blocked (liveness), generalizing across K and rebuild-vs-recure; falsified if two sessions both acquire the lease, a dead holder is never reclaimed, or a purely-content watermark already distinguishes in-flight from complete for every crash mode (lease then redundant). Head-to-head with H334
- **Experiment** - Neo4j-write concurrency + crash-injection harness on neo4j4 -> queues behind Phase-3; the MERGE+guard lease acquisition authored and unit-tested free now

**Group F - provenance in the graph (chunks, source, ingestion time, extractor)**

### R28-H325 DEFLATIONIST - per-assertion extractor stamp scopes re-extraction to the defective cohort

- **Grounding** - KGF records THAT an assertion changed (bitemporal valid_to, KGFEntityVersion) but never WHY; created_at/valid_from track time not extractor identity, so two versions run minutes apart are indistinguishable - the stamp is the join key that isolates the exact stale subset and disambiguates a valid_to set by a real-world change from one set by an extraction-quality fix. Stateless: the stamp is graph-resident scalar state on nodes/edges that already persist, so a foreign host rebuilds the which-extractor index by scanning properties, independent of the possibly-dangling Document.name pointer
- **Hypothesis** - every extracted entity and relationship must carry the identity of the extractor that produced it (model, version, prompt hash / a single extraction_fingerprint) so a stateless session can scope targeted re-extraction to exactly the assertions from a superseded or defective extractor version - a stamp absent from the entire graph today, and also an active upgrade trigger
- **Prediction** - baseline cannot scope repair so re-extraction is all-or-nothing; with the stamp a stateless session on a graph half-built under version A selects and re-extracts precisely the A-subset and zero current-version assertions, and on a B>A upgrade raises fidelity on the re-extracted cohort while leaving current assertions untouched
- **Acceptance bar** - a foreign session given only the graph identifies, re-extracts, and supersedes every prior-version assertion and zero current-version assertions on a held-out two-version graph, raising measured fidelity on the repaired cohort to match the source spans; falsified if extraction defects do NOT cluster by fingerprint (content-driven not extractor-driven) or fingerprints collide across genuinely different configs
- **Experiment** - the stamp is a loader SET-clause change (code + unit test, free now); the two-version re-extraction validation needs GPU + graph -> queues behind Phase-3

### R28-H326 CONTRARIAN - without recorded config, re-verification confounds error with extractor drift

- **Grounding** - targeted repair is a controlled experiment: to decide an assertion is wrong you must re-extract from its source chunk and diff, but the chunk text survives while the extractor that made the assertion does not, so a foreign host running a different model/prompt sees a confounded diff and cannot tell a hallucination from a newer extractor; recording the config makes re-extraction reproducible. Stateless: the fingerprint on the assertion/config node lets a fresh session reproduce the exact extractor from stored chunk text alone
- **Hypothesis** - assertions record WHICH chunk produced them but not HOW (model+version, prompt, chunk params), so a stateless foreign session's re-verification confounds genuine assertion error with extractor drift, producing false repairs; stamping the extraction-config fingerprint (or linking a shared :KGFExtractionConfig node keyed by config-hash) and re-extracting under the RECORDED config flags only discrepancies that survive config-matched re-extraction
- **Prediction** - baseline does 0 repairs; a config-blind audit flags at roughly the extractor-drift rate (mostly false); the config-matched audit drives false-repair rate toward ~0 while keeping true-error recall, on a corpus re-audited by a deliberately drifted extractor
- **Acceptance bar** - on a held-out corpus re-ingested by a foreign session with a deliberately swapped extractor, matched-config flags << blind-config flags and matched-config true-error recall is no worse than a same-config audit; falsified if re-extracting the same chunk under recorded vs drifted config yields statistically indistinguishable assertion sets
- **Experiment** - GPU + graph (drifted-extractor re-audit) -> queues behind Phase-3; the config-hash node schema authored free now

### R28-H327 FOLLOWER - reified DERIVED_FROM edge gives chunk-grain precision and dangling detection

- **Grounding** - a fidelity audit that finds one bad property on an entity mentioned in five chunks currently sees only a bag of five ids and must re-extract across all of them, and nothing guarantees the referenced chunk still exists (chunk_id is a content+index hash, so a re-chunk mints new nodes and orphans the old); a DERIVED_FROM edge points at the one chunk and makes dangling provenance a detectable broken edge. Stateless: the edge + Chunk.text are graph-resident so a session recovers exact lineage + integrity, and the merged SET cannot be de-aggregated once written
- **Hypothesis** - reifying each assertion's origin as a per-assertion DERIVED_FROM edge to its single originating chunk (vs the merged source_chunks SET), stamped with the extractor fingerprint, gives chunk-grain re-extraction precision AND makes dangling provenance a detectable broken edge under re-chunking
- **Prediction** - repair precision improves from N chunks to 1 (a seeded faulty description from chunk C3 of 5 is localized to C3); after a re-chunk the string-list design surfaces 0 integrity failures while DERIVED_FROM + scan surfaces N>0 dangling assertions
- **Acceptance bar** - on a graph-only host, localize a seeded fault to its single source chunk via DERIVED_FROM and re-extract only it (the four correct-source chunks never touched), and after a foreign re-chunk the scan identifies the orphaned-provenance set with high precision and recall; falsified if faulty assertions almost always derive from one chunk anyway AND chunk_ids are stable across every re-ingestion
- **Experiment** - graph + GPU (seeded fault localization + re-chunk) -> queues behind Phase-3; the DERIVED_FROM write + integrity scan authored free now

### R28-H328 HERETICAL - two provenance facts suffice; the source-file path must never be a dependency

- **Grounding** - a foreign stateless session on Neo4j can already recover the exact source span (MENTIONED_IN -> Chunk.text) and, with the stamp, which extractor produced it, and that pair is closed under audit, re-extraction, and lineage so any heavier lineage store is derivable on demand; the source-file path is not just redundant but hazardous - it dangles for a foreign host, silently breaking statelessness. Stateless: both facts live on nodes/edges already in Neo4j, and the banned file pointer is precisely the thing that would have dangled
- **Hypothesis** - for stateless repair the graph needs exactly two provenance facts - verbatim Chunk.text (already persisted) plus a per-assertion extractor stamp - and every richer lineage structure is deletable dead weight; all re-extraction routes to Chunk.text, never Document.path
- **Prediction** - delete all source files from disk, flag N assertions, re-extract using only the graph: success rate and re-extracted fidelity equal the file-based path within noise, while the baseline can neither locate the source nor re-extract (0 repairs); no repair/audit task in the KGF suite needs a provenance structure beyond {Chunk.text, MENTIONED_IN, extracted_by}
- **Acceptance bar** - graph-only re-extraction matches file-based re-extraction fidelity within measurement noise across every corpus document format, AND a survey of the repair/audit tasks finds none a separately-persisted lineage subgraph could serve but the two facts cannot; falsified by a concrete repair/audit task genuinely requiring provenance NOT reconstructable from verbatim chunk text + extractor stamp + MENTIONED_IN (e.g. a multi-hop assertion whose evidence spans chunks MENTIONED_IN cannot recover). Head-to-head with H327/H329
- **Experiment** - GPU + graph with source files deleted (the decisive stateless test) -> queues behind Phase-3; the repair/audit-task survey is a free code-read now

### R28-H329 DEFLATIONIST - provenance_kind routes each assertion to the right regenerator

- **Grounding** - a stateless router branches on provenance_kind: extracted -> re-run the extractor over the persisted text of source_chunks, derived -> re-run the named derivation over its current derived_from inputs; without the kind stamp the router either wrongly re-extracts a derived merge (which has no chunk to read) or wrongly leaves a stale derivation un-recomputed after its inputs changed. Stateless: provenance_kind + derived_from are graph-resident, extending an in-graph pattern (SAME_AS.method) that already survives statelessly
- **Hypothesis** - extracted and graph-derived assertions must be distinguishable by a provenance_kind stamp (plus derivation inputs for derived ones) because a stateless repair router cannot otherwise decide whether to regenerate an assertion from Chunk.text or from current graph state; today only SAME_AS edges carry this
- **Prediction** - baseline: a repair on a derived assertion (empty source_chunks) silently no-ops; with the stamp every assertion routes to the correct regenerator and the audit surfaces the count of derived assertions whose inputs have since changed as an explicit gap/abstention signal
- **Acceptance bar** - given a graph where a source document was re-ingested with a changed entity name, a stateless session regenerates the affected extracted assertions from Chunk.text AND recomputes the dependent derived SAME_AS/propositions from their inputs, with zero mis-routed repairs; falsified if every non-alias assertion already resolves to a correct regeneration procedure from existing properties
- **Experiment** - graph + GPU (mixed extracted/derived regeneration) -> queues behind Phase-3; the provenance_kind + derived_from schema (generalizing SAME_AS {method,evidence}) authored free now

### R28-H330 SCOUT - a write-ahead sentinel detects crash-torn writes indistinguishable from sparse docs

- **Grounding** - today _stable_load and write_control commit in separate transactions and no node records how many chunks a document's extraction intended to emit, so a death between commits leaves either a duplicate re-ingest or a silently partial document indistinguishable from a sparse one; an OPEN sentinel keyed to a now-stale lease is unambiguous physical evidence of a torn write. Stateless: the sentinel IS graph-resident state, a foreign host reads OPEN units + the KGFLock lease to decide what to repair, no local files
- **Hypothesis** - a per-ingest-unit write-ahead sentinel ((:KGFIngestUnit {fingerprint,run_id,expected_chunks,status}), OPEN before entity writes, SEALED in the same transaction as the final commit with observed==expected) is the one signal that lets a stateless session detect and repair crash-torn writes; on start it scans stale-OPEN units, deletes the partial run_id entities, and re-extracts
- **Prediction** - inject a mid-document kill (crash after N of M chunk-entity writes, before seal) across a 10-doc corpus: baseline resumes with a duplicated or silently-truncated document and flags 0; the sentinel detects 100% of injected torn writes on next start and restores entity/type counts to within extraction variance of an uninterrupted run
- **Acceptance bar** - every injected torn write detected and repaired to a graph whose entity set and type-frequency vector match an uninterrupted single-pass ingest within variance, across kill points spanning first-chunk, mid-document, and post-entity-pre-fingerprint, with zero torn writes leaking into downstream drift/Chao1 baselines; falsified if a resumed session can already classify torn-vs-complete from existing persisted state alone
- **Experiment** - graph + kill-injection harness (+ GPU re-extract) -> queues behind Phase-3; a free code-read confirms _stable_load and the fingerprint write are currently separate transactions now

**Group G - statelessness solvability (can graph-only-state maintenance be solved?)**

### R28-H331 HERETICAL - the drift window is a pure fold over the bitemporal graph, no protocol needed

- **Grounding** - temporal.py's confirmed invalidate-never-delete doctrine means every entity/type/edge's entry and retraction time is recorded losslessly, so the type histogram at any past checkpoint is a function of the current graph; the baseline fired 52/0 solely because its window lived in RAM and reset each session, and folding the persisted temporal stamps makes the 3-consecutive counter cross session boundaries for free. Stateless: zero state beyond the graph, every foreign session recomputes the identical series deterministically because it is a pure function of the immutable temporal stamps
- **Hypothesis** - the drift/metric window is not lost state needing a reconstruction protocol but a pure deterministic fold over the never-deleted bitemporal graph, so a stateless session recomputes it with zero extra state and reproduces the stateful verdict; the persisted snapshot ledger (H315) and any lock/protocol are unnecessary
- **Prediction** - a stateless session re-derives the JSD series byte-for-byte matching the stateful session's emitted curing.metrics values and fires >=1 recure across a session boundary where the baseline fired 0 (the js=0.5079 owed recure)
- **Acceptance bar** - reconstructed JSD at every historical checkpoint matches the logged stateful values within float tolerance AND the 3-consecutive trigger fires at the same graph state a stateful-plus-persistent oracle says it should, verified across an artificial session restart mid-campaign; falsified by any merge/remap/dedup that rewrites or hard-deletes pre-merge nodes so the re-derived JSD diverges (the fold is then lossy and H315's snapshot ledger is vindicated) - this falsifier IS the test of the genuinely-unrecoverable set. Head-to-head with H315/H332
- **Experiment** - FREE replay, no GPU: recompute the type histogram at each historical boundary from the bitemporal stamps in the logs, fold JSD + the 3-consecutive rule, diff vs the logged stateful series; a live-graph confirmation of the merge/remap loss case queues behind Phase-3

### R28-H332 CONTRARIAN - drift metrics are trajectory functions; recompute-from-state is unfaithful

- **Grounding** - JSD-between-consecutive-waves and rolling variances depend on the order and micro-batching of ingestion and on intermediate type distributions that only ever existed transiently; these statistics are non-associative, so re-windowing at document/chunk granularity yields a different JSD sequence than the live per-wave sequence and the 3-consecutive trigger fires at different points - persisting the scalar at emit time captures trajectory information exactly once. Stateless: the trajectory is frozen into append-only nodes at the one moment it is observable, so a foreign host reconstructs an identical window by reading nodes, never by re-deriving path-dependent statistics
- **Hypothesis** - drift metrics are functions of the ingestion TRAJECTORY (order, micro-batching, transient intermediate distributions), not of final graph state, so a stateless session that recomputes the window from the final bitemporal graph will NOT reproduce the live verdict; the only faithful fix is to persist each metric snapshot at compute time (directly discriminates against H331 via ordering invariance)
- **Prediction** - a stateless session rehydrating from snapshot nodes reproduces the stateful trigger on >=95% of wave boundaries, whereas a session recomputing purely from bitemporal graph state diverges on the trigger for at least one wave in a corpus where ingestion order was permuted
- **Acceptance bar** - snapshot-rehydrated verdict == live stateful verdict on every wave AND invariant to at least two distinct ingestion orderings of the same document set, with the recompute-from-state arm visibly breaking the ordering invariance on the same corpus; falsified if recompute-from-state reproduces the live trigger within tolerance across permuted orderings (drift is then state-determined, H331 wins). THE DECISIVE DISCRIMINATOR - runs first in group G
- **Experiment** - FREE replay, no GPU: reconstruct both the snapshot-rehydrated and the recompute-from-state windows from the logs, then re-run under >=2 permuted document orderings of the same set and compare trigger points (the permuted-order simulation is offline arithmetic over the logged per-doc metric vectors)

### R28-H333 DEFLATIONIST - reconstruction is solvable except one missing datum, the cured ontology reference

- **Grounding** - remap_rate is a pure function of (loaded entity types, Document provenance, cured type-set); the first two already persist and the third is the only transient (it lives only in the seeded cured_frequencies passed to __init__), and the invalidation/contradiction rate is already a pure function of bitemporal edges - once the cured reference is pinned the last-N window is a deterministic replay of committed state. Stateless: a foreign session reads the OntologySnapshot node plus committed Documents and bitemporal edges, replays, and reaches the same verdict with no local files
- **Hypothesis** - reconstruction of the drift signal is fully solvable EXCEPT one genuinely-missing datum - the cured ontology reference (type-set + reference frequencies) which never persists and without which remap_rate and the JSD baseline are undefined; writing one OntologySnapshot at the FSM STABLE transition (cured type-set + reference frequencies + phase + cure fingerprint) closes the unrecoverable set
- **Prediction** - baseline 52/0 at js=0.5079; with the snapshot present a stateless detector replays all 11 logs as one continuous window and emits >=1 recure/rebuild at the sustained-JSD stretch, per-document verdict matching a reference continuous-window run exactly, holding on a held-out corpus with a different cure point
- **Acceptance bar** - round-trip identity at every document step between graph-reconstructed and continuous-window stateful verdict, AND >=1 recure/rebuild the per-session baseline missed, generalizing to a different cure point; falsified if with the OntologySnapshot present the reconstructed verdict still diverges at any step (a further hidden state variable is then genuinely unrecoverable). The honest middle of the H331-vs-H332 tension
- **Experiment** - FREE replay, no GPU: recover the cured type-set from the first curing.cured event in each log (the OntologySnapshot proxy), recompute remap + per-doc type-frequencies by ordered replay, feed the unchanged _evaluate, diff vs a reference continuous run

### R28-H334 HERETICAL - content-addressed ActionClaim gives at-most-once actuation, no liveness assumption

- **Grounding** - two stateless sessions past the rebuild threshold both decide to act; a lease closes the race only while its holder lives, and a session that dies after acquiring leaves a lock no one can safely reclaim (a dead holder is indistinguishable from a slow one without external state), whereas Neo4j serializes writes on a MERGE so a content-addressed claim gives at-most-once per distinct graph state and a restarted session recomputes the SAME key and correctly sees the action already claimed. Stateless: the action key is derived entirely from in-graph state (fingerprint + persisted snapshot signature), so any foreign session recomputes the identical key and converges at-most-once with zero carried state - the R26 external ledger moving in-graph, made safe by content-addressing
- **Hypothesis** - for actuation under concurrent foreign stateless writers a lease is the WRONG primitive (holder-addressed, a stateless session can die mid-lease leaving a stuck lock); a content-addressed idempotent ActionClaim keyed on hash(graph_fingerprint + threshold-crossing signature), MERGEd atomically, gives at-most-once execution per distinct graph state with no liveness assumption (R26's H274 answer cache collapses into the same discipline)
- **Prediction** - two concurrent stateless sessions past the rebuild threshold produce exactly ONE rebuild with the ActionClaim MERGE; in the injected mid-action-death scenario the lease design produces two rebuilds (holder died before writing the lease) or zero forward progress (stuck lease), while ActionClaim still yields exactly one and self-heals on restart
- **Acceptance bar** - exactly-once rebuild per distinct graph fingerprint under (a) two simultaneous live sessions and (b) a session killed immediately after claiming but before completing (case b must complete or safely re-claim without operator intervention, with a lease-based arm shown to stall or double-act in case b); falsified if an explicit TTL lease reliably yields exactly-once AND self-recovers from mid-action death without external state, or if Neo4j MERGE does not serialize concurrent claims to one winner. Head-to-head with H324
- **Experiment** - Neo4j-write concurrency + mid-action-kill harness on neo4j4 -> queues behind Phase-3, on the same harness as H324; the action-key derivation authored and unit-tested free now

### R28-H335 SCOUT - a quiescence barrier makes a stateless rebuild bounded-consistent, never instantaneous

- **Grounding** - the current lease guarantees mutual exclusion of two WRITERS but not read-snapshot consistency for a reader-that-rewrites; a rebuild reads the whole graph, recomputes types/merges, and writes back, so a sealed wave N immediately followed by a committing wave N+1 gives the rebuild a torn snapshot straddling both, and a quiescence barrier keyed on "no OPEN wave" restores snapshot consistency. Stateless: quiescence is decided by reading KGFWave OPEN/SEALED state + the lease-role node, so a foreign session queries the graph to learn whether it is safe to rebuild, establishing the honest boundary "bounded-consistent after one TTL"
- **Hypothesis** - instantaneous cross-session consistency for a destructive rebuild is irreducibly unrecoverable (the single ingest lock is a liveness fence, not a snapshot barrier), so a rebuild from a stateless session can snapshot a graph a concurrent ingest extends microseconds later; a maintenance-role lease requiring quiescence (no OPEN KGFWave, no live ingest lease, blocks new waves while held) converts this from silently-stale to bounded-consistent after one TTL
- **Prediction** - a constructible history exists where a rebuild under the plain single-slot lease yields a fingerprint different from the same rebuild under wave-quiescence (a demonstrable staleness gap), while the quiescence-barred rebuild fingerprint equals a frozen-graph rebuild; the baseline never rebuilds at all
- **Acceptance bar** - (a) plain-lease rebuild fingerprint != frozen-graph fingerprint under a concurrent sealed-then-new-wave interleaving (staleness gap is real) AND (b) quiescence-barred rebuild fingerprint == frozen-graph fingerprint across the same interleavings (not one hand-picked schedule); falsified if a plain-lease rebuild fingerprint ALWAYS equals frozen (barrier unnecessary), or if even the quiescence-barred rebuild diverges (the limit lies deeper than a lock node). Depends on H330's wave-seal marker (H339 tests the marker itself)
- **Experiment** - Neo4j-write + concurrent-wave interleaving harness on neo4j4 -> queues behind Phase-3, sharing H330's wave substrate

### R28-H336 CONTRARIAN - in-graph answer cache fidelity reopens H273-vs-H274 under the stateless kernel

- **Grounding** - R26 pre-registered the in-graph derived layer (H273) against the external answer cache (H274) and H274 WON (identical safety at zero graph coupling), but that verdict assumed an EXTERNAL store a stateful session owns; under the stateless kernel a foreign session cannot see an external cache, so H334 moves the cache in-graph as content-addressed nodes and the question H274's external verdict never had to answer surfaces: does an in-graph answer cache stay FAITHFUL (invalidate correctly when its supporting facts change) without the external store's freedom to just drop entries. Stateless: the answer node + its dependency edges are graph-resident, a foreign session validates freshness by a 1-hop check on the dependency edges' valid_to, never an external cache lookup
- **Hypothesis** - an in-graph answer node keyed by (question-fingerprint + supporting-fact-version-set), with a bitemporal dependency edge to each input assertion, is invalidated correctly when any input is superseded, so a stateless session never serves an answer whose inputs have changed, and this in-graph cache matches H274's external hit-rate/safety at zero external state - inverting the R26 head-to-head under statelessness
- **Prediction** - content-addressing on the input-version-set makes staleness self-detecting (a changed input mints a new key, the old answer bitemporally closes), so the in-graph cache serves 0 stale answers and is SAFER than the external one under concurrent writers at H274's hit-rate within noise
- **Acceptance bar** - on a replay where a supporting fact is superseded mid-stream the in-graph cache serves 0 stale answers (every dependency edge correctly invalidates) AND hit-rate within noise of H274's external number; falsified if the in-graph cache serves a stale answer (dependency tracking incomplete) or its invalidation requires a full-graph scan a stateless session cannot afford per-query (external H274 then still wins even under statelessness, exposing a genuine cost of the kernel)
- **Experiment** - FREE replay over the R26 usage-coupling-gate artifacts + query-answer logs (reconstruct H274's external result, simulate the in-graph dependency-edge invalidation, compare stale-serve counts); the Neo4j dependency-edge invalidation validates behind Phase-3

### R28-H337 DEFLATIONIST - the calibration artifact must reconstruct from the graph or every merge decays

- **Grounding** - v28 ships a calibration artifact (2-point isotonic curve, 25 ground-truth pairs, 44% accuracy) that maps raw posterior -> calibrated probability, but it lives as a file object (calibration_path), not in the graph, so a stateless session with no local files loses it and the resolver falls back to raw miscalibrated posteriors, silently degrading every merge decision - symmetric to the drift-window loss (H315/H331). Stateless: isotonic knots + ground-truth pairs as :CalibrationPair nodes let a foreign session rebuild the calibration map by reading them, no calibration_path file
- **Hypothesis** - persisting the calibration artifact in-graph (isotonic knots + the 25 ground-truth pairs as nodes) lets a stateless session reconstruct the exact calibration map and reproduce the merge/defer decisions a calibrated stateful session makes; round-trip fidelity
- **Prediction** - a stateless session reading the in-graph calibration reproduces the calibrated posterior for every pair within float tolerance and makes byte-identical merge/block/defer decisions, while a stateless session WITHOUT it (raw-posterior fallback) flips >=1 decision - demonstrating the artifact is load-bearing and its in-graph persistence necessary
- **Acceptance bar** - calibrated-decision parity between graph-reconstructed and stateful-with-file across the H101 298-pair + H157 frozen sets, AND >=1 decision flip when the artifact is absent (proving it matters); falsified if raw-posterior and calibrated decisions are identical on the frozen pairs (the calibration artifact then changes no decision and its statelessness is moot)
- **Experiment** - FREE replay, no GPU: reconstruct calibrated vs raw decisions offline over the frozen H101 benchmark + the v28 calibration artifact + the resolution.merge posteriors in the logs; the :CalibrationPair + isotonic-knot in-graph write validates behind Phase-3

### R28-H338 FOLLOWER - demand-ledger round-trip: is the usage signal graph-reconstructable

- **Grounding** - group G proves the DRIFT signal is graph-reconstructable (H331/H333); the symmetric question for the DEMAND/usage signal (H271 demand ledger, H321 Demand nodes) is untested - can a stateless session reconstruct the repair-budget state from the graph and reproduce the allocation a stateful session with the formerly-external ledger would make. Stateless: Demand nodes carry the counts, a foreign session ranks repairs by reading them, no external ledger
- **Hypothesis** - with the demand ledger in-graph (Demand nodes carrying query-fingerprint + hit-count + last-seen-version), a stateless session reconstructs the identical repair-budget allocation (which gaps get repaired first) a stateful session produced from the external H271 ledger; round-trip fidelity on the allocation ranking, and the H275 recurrence-gate also reconstructs from the persisted counts
- **Prediction** - the in-graph demand reconstruction reproduces the H271 top-K repair allocation exactly (same gaps, same order) because the ranking is a pure function of persisted hit-counts + gap statistics
- **Acceptance bar** - allocation-ranking parity (same top-K gaps, Kendall-tau >=0.9) between graph-reconstructed and the H271 external-ledger allocation on the R26 usage-coupling replay; falsified if the reconstructed allocation diverges (a usage signal is lost) OR the external and in-graph allocations never differ because demand does not actually reorder repairs (the ledger is decorative)
- **Experiment** - FREE replay over the R26 usage-coupling-gates artifacts (demand ledger events + gap ledger): reconstruct the in-graph Demand allocation offline, compare to the recorded external allocation; the in-graph Demand node writes validate behind Phase-3

### R28-H339 SCOUT - the wave-seal marker's own correctness is the foundation H330 and H335 assume

- **Grounding** - H330 (torn-write repair) and H335 (quiescence-barrier rebuild) both ASSUME the OPEN/SEALED wave-seal marker is correct - that SEALED reliably means all writes committed and OPEN reliably means incomplete - but neither tests the marker itself; if the seal can flip SEALED before the last write commits (or stay OPEN after a clean finish) both dependent arms inherit a false signal. Stateless: the marker IS graph-resident and a foreign session trusts SEALED/OPEN as its torn-write and quiescence signal, so the marker's own correctness is the foundation of stateless repair safety
